_source line 960 · 2026-05-25T21:36:47.378Z_

```json
{
  "type": "queue-operation",
  "operation": "enqueue",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-25T21:36:47.378Z",
  "sessionId": "78cfbf9f-97b6-46ed-a5de-e1517a0d03f9",
  "content": "[审稿报告]\nOverall: 3.5 (Borderline Conference)\n\n# Round 20 审稿报告 — Temporal Score Incomparability in LLM Benchmarks\n\n**论文**: Temporal Score Incomparability in LLM Benchmarks: A Large-Scale Psychometric Audit of 5,227 Models\n**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 (ARR May cycle)\n**Reviewer 配置**: 2 人（R1: 方法严谨性+实验有效性; R2: 新颖性/定位+完整性）\n**审稿轮次**: Round 1（1 轮收敛，无需第 2 轮）\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n本文将教育测量学中的 Mantel-Haenszel 差异项目功能（MH-DIF）分析引入 LLM 基准测试的时序可比性检验，基于 METABENCH 响应矩阵（5,227 模型 × 12,508 MMLU 题目）发现约 29–31% 的题目在 2023 与 2024 模型群组间呈现大效应 DIF（ETS C 类），且该信号与 web-overlap 污染标签低相关（φ = 0.012）。论文开发了 cross-subject matching 半合成验证框架（ΔFPR = 0 构造性保证），在六个基准上复现了发现（17.9–31.3%），并提出 Monitor/Triage/Correct 审计协议。\n\n---\n\n## Strengths\n\n**S1. 问题定位精准且及时。** [R1-S1, R2-S1] 将 measurement invariance 引入 LLM 评估填补了重要方法论空白。5,227 模型 × 12,508 题目 × 6 基准的规模在同类工作中罕见，核心发现在所有基准上复现（Table 2: C% 17.9–31.3%）。\n\n**S2. Cross-subject matching 设计巧妙。** [R1-S3, R2-S3] 将 purified subtest matching 适配到 LLM 多学科场景，ΔFPR = 0 的构造性保证（Section 3.4 Remark）是方法论亮点。Figure 5 清晰展示 FPR 控制差异。\n\n**S3. 多层次控制实验设计严谨。** [R1-S4, R2-S2] 五种 cohort 对比、DEFF 校正、cluster bootstrap、LOFO 家族分析、random-split baseline（0.62% vs 31.3% = 50×）、IRT θ-conditioning（r = 0.9986）从多角度排除混淆。\n\n**S4. 概念框架有教育意义。** [R2-S4] Figure 2 通过 ICC 曲线清晰展示 global exposure vs differential functioning 区别，对 NLP 评估社区有启发价值。\n\n**S5. Base-vs-instruct 方向反转是有价值的发现。** [R2-S6] χ² = 562.7 的结构化方向性差异超越简单二分法，指向训练方法论对测量特性的系统性影响。\n\n**S6. 心理测量概念解释充分。** [R2-S5, R1-S6] Section 3.2 对 MH/ETS/DIF 概念的铺垫和 Figure 1 pipeline 图对 NLP 读者友好。\n\n---\n\n## Weaknesses\n\n### Must-Fix（影响接收的关键问题）\n\n**W1. [Critical, Consensus] 标题 \"Score Incomparability\" 与核心发现 ρ ≈ 0.997 存在根本性叙事张力。**\n标题暗示分数不可比较，但核心实证发现恰恰是排名几乎完美保持（Table 2: ρ ≈ 0.997, flip rate ≤ 2.9%）。Introduction \"The result is sobering\" 放大了此不匹配。论文实际展示的是 item-level instability with aggregate robustness——31% 的题目有大效应 DIF，但聚合排名几乎不变。虽然个别模型可移动 ±561 位（Appendix），DIF-ordered removal 比 random removal 更快降低 ρ（Figure 6），但 aggregate 层面的近乎完美保持使 \"incomparability\" 一词明显过度。\n- R1: AGREE | R2: 原始提出\n- **跨轮状态**: 新发现（R20 首次提出）\n- **建议**: 标题改为更准确的措辞（如 \"Temporal Item-Level Instability\" 或 \"Differential Item Functioning\"）；弱化 \"sobering\" 等措辞\n\n**W2. [Critical, Consensus] 核心 C% 数字的不确定性范围过大（13.5%–31.3%），且 Abstract 使用未校正值。**\nAbstract 使用 \"31%\" 作为 headline claim，但 DEFF 校正后 simple-mean = 29.2%、weighted-mean = 13.5%，2.3× 范围（Section 4.2, Appendix D Table 10）。Cluster bootstrap CI [26.7%, 53.2%] 排除 weighted-mean 的 13.5%，但 bootstrap 依赖于 family 定义的合理性（Appendix I: 30 families with ≥2 members），论文未提供 family 定义的敏感性分析。如果真实 C% 接近 13.5%，\"50× above random\" 变为 ~22×，叙事力度显著减弱。\n- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE\n- **跨轮状态**: R19-N1 再次出现。R19→R20 action（改用 29.2% + CI）标记 done，但 Abstract 仍使用 raw \"31%\"\n- **建议**: Abstract 使用 DEFF 校正后数字（~29% 或报告区间）；提供 family 定义敏感性分析收窄区间\n\n**W3. [Critical, Divergence] 方法论新颖性有限——核心方法均为已知技术的直接应用/适配。**\nMH-DIF (Holland & Thayer, 1988)、ETS A/B/C 分类、cross-subject matching (purified subtest matching, Swaminathan & Rogers, 1990)、DEFF 校正 (Rao & Scott, 1992) 均非原创。论文的四个 LLM-specific challenges（Section 3.2）的解决方案也是现有方法（permutation simulation、DEFF、quintile stratification、cross-subject matching）。\n- R1: 部分 REBUT——cross-subject matching 适配、semi-synthetic injection framework、四个 LLM-specific challenges 的系统性解决是 non-trivial 的设计创新；对 EMNLP 而言，成熟方法严谨适配到新领域并通过大规模实证验证本身有价值\n- R2: 原始提出——NLP 顶会对方法论创新要求高于\"已知方法到新领域的迁移\"\n- **跨轮状态**: R19-D1 分歧持续\n- **建议**: Introduction 更清晰区分 borrowed methodology 与 novel adaptations；标注哪些是已知方法、哪些是本文的适配创新\n\n### Should-Fix（显著影响论文质量）\n\n**W4. [Major, Consensus] \"Mechanism-agnostic\" 定位与 contamination-centric 验证/叙事存在张力。**\n论文声称 DIF 是 mechanism-agnostic（Section 5.1: \"Temporal DIF detects *that* items function differently...not *why*\"），但：(1) 验证框架完全基于 contamination injection（Section 3.4）；(2) Section 4.3 大量对比 web-overlap labels；(3) Discussion（Section 5.2）建议 contamination-motivated item removal。如果方法真正 mechanism-agnostic，至少应包含一个非 contamination 来源的 DIF 验证（如模拟训练方法变化）。\n- R1: AGREE | R2: 原始提出\n- **跨轮状态**: R19-W3 再次出现。Related Work 重排已完成，但验证框架仍单一\n- **建议**: 补充至少一个非 contamination 来源的 DIF 验证\n\n**W5. [Major, Consensus] Semi-synthetic 注入使用 uniform binary flipping，可能高估检测力。**\n论文在 Limitations (1) 和 Section 3.4 已承认此局限（\"reported ΔTPR values may overestimate detection power\"），Section 4.4 的 difficulty-dependent injection 量化了 ~20% 高估（ΔTPR ≈ 0.41 vs 0.51）。但验证框架仅覆盖 contamination 注入的不同变体，未覆盖其他 DIF 机制。\n- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE（指出论文已有部分回应，~20% bound）\n- **跨轮状态**: R19-N2 再次出现。Reframe as calibration upper bound 已完成\n- **建议**: 考虑补充 graded injection 实验\n\n**W6. [Major, Consensus] ETS 阈值从人类考生迁移到 LLM 缺乏 substantive meaning 讨论。**\nETS A/B/C 阈值校准于人类教育测试群体。LLM 无真正 latent ability trait；within-family 高同质性 inflate sample size 但不增独立信息。跨阈值鲁棒性分析（Section 4.2: 18.4% at |Δ_MH| ≥ 2.0, 10.2% at ≥ 2.5）部分缓解阈值选择的任意性，但论文未讨论 |Δ_MH| = 1.5 对 LLM 的实质含义，也未提供 LLM-specific 阈值校准。\n- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE\n- **跨轮状态**: R19-W4 再次出现。已连接 Monte Carlo power + random-split FPR，但 reviewer 要求更深入\n- **建议**: 添加 ETS 阈值在 LLM 场景下实质含义的讨论段落\n\n**W7. [Major, Divergence] DIF 在 LLM 场景下的概念合法性讨论不足。**\n2023 vs 2024 模型不是同一总体的人口学子群，而是技术上根本不同的系统。κ = 0.80（cross-subject vs within-subject matching agreement）意味着 20% 的 DIF 项目可能是多维性伪影。需讨论：何时 LLM temporal DIF 反映测量问题（item 有问题）vs 合法能力结构变化（model 本来就不同）。\n- R1: 部分 REBUT——DIF 对跨时间群体也有效（教育测量先例），Q3 diagnostic + cross-subject matching 部分缓解多维性\n- R2: 原始提出\n- **建议**: 补充讨论 LLM temporal DIF 的概念边界\n\n**W8. [Major, Consensus] Base-vs-instruct 反向模式（χ² = 562.7）机制解释不足。**\nRLHF/instruction tuning 系统性地改变模型与 MCQ 的交互方式（format preference, safety behavior, calibration），这些都可能产生 DIF-like artifacts 而非真正的 item-level bias。论文在 Section 5.1 将反转解释为 \"structured item-level bias rather than uniform group impact\"，但未排除上述 confound。Appendix O (Table 15) 的 base-only/instruct-only temporal DIF 分析应被提升到主文以回应此质疑。\n- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE\n- **跨轮状态**: 新发现（R20 首次提出）\n- **建议**: 讨论 format sensitivity 作为替代解释；将 Table 15 提升到主文\n\n**W9. [Major, Partial Consensus] Cross-subject ΔFPR = 0 保证在 k = 5–15 区间退化未充分表征。**\nk ≤ 5 ΔFPR < 0.007，k = 20 ΔFPR = 0.167。中间区域缺乏系统刻画，对 practitioner 判断何时切换 within-subject matching 至关重要。\n- R1: 原始提出 | R2: 部分 AGREE（\"加分项\"而非\"否决性\"缺陷）\n- **跨轮状态**: R19-W2 再次出现\n- **建议**: 补充 k = 5, 8, 10, 15 的 ΔFPR 数据点\n\n**W10. [Moderate, Consensus] \"近独立于 web-overlap labels\" 的推断强度过高。**\nφ = 0.012 可能反映 web-overlap labels 的 proxy 噪声而非 DIF 的真正独立性。论文已承认此可能性（Section 4.3），并提供 label-independent 支持证据（base-vs-instruct reversal），但 \"complementary diagnostic dimensions\" 措辞仍过强。\n- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE\n- **建议**: 弱化措辞为 \"low correlation with available contamination proxies\"\n\n**W11. [Minor, Consensus] Contribution 3 (audit protocol) 权重不足。**\nSection 5.2 仅半段，无部署验证、case study 或用户研究。作为声明的三大贡献之一显得单薄。\n- R1: AGREE | R2: 原始提出\n- **建议**: 降级为 future direction，或扩展并提供模拟部署场景\n\n---\n\n## Reviewer Consensus\n\n[AGREE] 双方一致同意的关键问题（7 项）：\n1. 标题 \"Score Incomparability\" 与 ρ ≈ 0.997 的叙事不匹配 (W1)\n2. C% 不确定性和 Abstract headline claim 表述 (W2)\n3. Mechanism-agnostic 定位与 contamination 验证的张力 (W4)\n4. Base-vs-instruct 反转的机制解释不足 (W8)\n5. ETS 阈值迁移的实质含义讨论不足 (W6)\n6. Web-overlap labels 推断强度过高 (W10)\n7. Contribution 3 权重不足 (W11)\n\n## Reviewer Divergence\n\n[REBUT/Partial] 存在分歧的问题（4 项）：\n\n1. **方法论新颖性** (W3): R2 认为核心方法为直接搬运（Critical），R1 反驳 cross-subject matching 适配和 semi-synthetic framework 是 non-trivial 的设计创新。分歧未收敛。\n\n2. **2PL power analysis vs IRT misfit** (R1-W3): R2 反驳——MH-DIF 是非参数方法，power analysis 是辅助证据，实证 50× ratio 是核心。R1 接受但建议论文更明确讨论 2PL misfit 影响。**降级为 minor concern。**\n\n3. **多重比较校正** (R1-W6): R2 反驳——DIF 领域标准实践不做 FWER/FDR correction（Wainer 1993），BH 校正仅影响 3/3918 C items (0.08%)。**降级为 minor concern。**\n\n4. **DIF 概念合法性** (W7): R1 部分反驳——DIF 对跨时间群体有效（教育测量先例），Q3 diagnostic 部分缓解多维性。但同意需更多讨论。\n\n---\n\n## Questions\n\n1. Abstract 是否应使用 DEFF 校正后数字（~29% + CI）而非 raw 31%？为何选择 simple-mean 而非 weighted-mean DEFF？\n2. 能否提供 family 定义的敏感性分析以收窄 C% 区间？\n3. 既然 ρ ≈ 0.997，temporal DIF 的 practical impact 是什么？在什么具体场景下 practitioner 会因忽略 temporal DIF 做出错误决策？\n4. DIF 信号是线性积累还是存在突变点？Rolling-window MH-DIF 分析结果如何？\n5. Base-only / instruct-only temporal DIF (Table 15) 为何不在主文展示？\n6. 如果补充非 contamination 来源的 DIF 验证（如训练方法变化模拟），结果如何？\n7. |Δ_MH| = 1.5 对 LLM 的实质含义是什么？能否提供 LLM-specific 阈值校准？\n8. Cross-subject matching 在全局训练方法变化驱动 DIF 时的行为如何？（R1 论证为 conservative bias，不导致 false positive inflation，但可能 under-powered）\n\n---\n\n## Missing References\n\n1. **Rodriguez et al. (2021) \"Evaluation Examples Are Not Equally Informative\" (ACL 2021)** — IRT+NLP 评估的奠基性工作 [R1 AGREE, R2 提出]\n2. **Polo et al. (2024) \"tinyBenchmarks\" (ICML 2024)** — IRT-based 高效 LLM 评估 [R2 提出; R1 部分 REBUT: 技术路线差异大]\n3. **Vania et al. (2021) \"Comparing Test Sets with Item Response Theory\"** — NLP+IRT 早期工作 [R1 提出]\n4. **van der Linden & Veldkamp (2007)** — Item exposure / test security 文献 [R1 提出; R2: nice-to-have]\n\n---\n\n## External Review (Stanford Agentic Reviewer)\n\n外部审稿：未配置，不适用。\n\n---\n\n## Visual Layout Check（非评分项）\n\n视觉审查已完成。7 个 info 级别发现，0 critical，0 warning：\n\n- **Page 2**: Figure 1 response matrix 彩色小点灰度打印辨识度不足，建议辅以 marker shape 或 hatching\n- **Page 4**: Figure 3 ETS 颜色编码（A=绿, B=黄, C=红）对红绿色盲不友好，建议改用 Okabe-Ito 色板或 blue-orange 方案\n- **Page 5**: Figure 4 x 轴标签字号偏小，部分重叠，建议调整间距或旋转\n- **Page 7**: Figure 6 五个面板 tick/legend 文字偏小（约正文 60-65%），建议增大字号\n- **Page 8**: 右栏内容密度极高（§4.5–4.7 + §5 + §6），无 display item 穿插，但作为 Discussion/Conclusion 区域属正常安排\n- **Page 13**: Tables 6–8 密集排布，单个表格行数少（3-5 行），可考虑合并\n- **Page 14**: Table 10 列头缩写较多，建议 caption 解释\n\n---\n\n## Overall Assessment\n\n**Overall Score: 3.5 (Borderline Conference)**\n\n| 维度 | R1 | R2 | 综合 |\n|------|----|----|------|\n| Soundness | 3/5 | 4/5 | 3.5/5 |\n| Excitement | 3.5/5 | 3/5 | 3.0/5 |\n| Reproducibility | 4/5 | — | 4/5 |\n| Confidence | 4/5 | 4/5 | 4/5 |\n\n**综合评判**: 这是一篇执行质量高的大规模实证论文，问题重要且及时，多层次验证设计严谨，cross-subject matching 的 ΔFPR = 0 保证有方法论价值。但存在三个核心障碍阻碍其升至 Conference (4.0)：\n\n1. **叙事过度**: 标题 \"Score Incomparability\" 与 ρ ≈ 0.997 的发现直接矛盾；Abstract 使用未校正的 31% 而非 DEFF 校正后的区间\n2. **方法论新颖性争议**: 核心统计方法均为 30+ 年历史的标准工具，LLM-specific 适配的创新程度在两位 reviewer 间有分歧\n3. **验证框架单一**: 声称 mechanism-agnostic 但验证完全基于 contamination injection\n\n**升至 Conference (4.0) 的路径** (rebuttal 可解决)：\n- (a) 修改标题和 Abstract，使用 DEFF 校正后数字，诚实反映不确定性\n- (b) 在 Introduction 中明确区分 borrowed methodology 与 novel adaptations\n- (c) 补充至少一个非 contamination DIF 机制的验证\n- (d) 讨论 ETS 阈值在 LLM 场景下的 substantive meaning\n- (e) 提供 family 定义敏感性分析以收窄 C% 区间\n- (f) 将 base-only/instruct-only temporal DIF (Table 15) 提升到主文\n\n**作为 Findings 论文** (3.0–3.5): 论文完全达标——规模大、执行扎实、对社区有诊断价值。\n\n---\n\n## Detailed Comments\n\n**Abstract**: 信息密度适中（~150 words，符合 ≤200 words 要求），但 \"31%\" headline 未反映 DEFF 校正后的不确定性。\"near-independent\" 措辞过强。\n\n**Introduction (Section 1)**: Figure 1 pipeline 图信息量大，四步管线清晰。三个 contributions 权重不均：C3 (audit protocol) 后续 Section 5.2 仅半段，缺乏部署验证。\"The result is sobering\" 与 ρ ≈ 0.997 的 aggregate robustness 不一致。\n\n**Related Work (Section 2)**: 组织合理（Psychometric Methods → Contamination Detection → Benchmark Quality）。与 Growing Pains 的区分清晰（nonparametric vs parametric, DIF diagnosis vs score equating）。遗漏 Rodriguez et al. (2021)。\n\n**Method (Section 3)**: MH-DIF 和 ETS 分类的解释清晰，包括公式推导（Eq. 1-2）和四个 LLM-specific challenges 的讨论。Cross-subject matching 的 Remark 论证有说服力。Semi-synthetic injection 的 uniform flipping 局限性在 Limitations 中承认，但 Section 3.4 正文措辞不够 cautious——应在首次提出时标注 \"calibration upper bound\"。\n\n**Experiments (Section 4)**: 多层次控制设计严谨。Section 4.2 的 DEFF 区间处理（\"bracketing C% at 13.5–29.2%\"）不够 transparent——应在主文而非仅附录讨论 estimator 选择。Section 4.3 的 web-overlap 分析有 proxy noise confound。Section 4.2 \"DIF rates are domain-invariant (KW p = 0.78)\" 与 \"DIF direction exhibits domain dependence\" 的并置需更清晰铺垫。\n\n**Discussion (Section 5)**: Section 5.1 的 base-vs-instruct 分析有价值但机制解释不足——需讨论 format sensitivity 作为替代解释。Section 5.2 audit protocol 过于简短。\n\n**Conclusion (Section 6)**: 单句 conclusion 信息量可接受但给人仓促收尾之感。\n\n**Limitations**: 覆盖 3 个关键局限（injection realism, non-independence, cross-subject matching boundary），符合 EMNLP 要求。\n\n**Appendix**: A–Q (17 个) 内容详尽，但数量较多，可考虑合并部分相关性强的 appendix。\n\n**Format**: 正文 8 页内符合限制；Limitations 在第 9 页不计入页数；匿名链接 anonymous.4open.science 格式正确；引用格式符合 ACL 规范。\n---\n\n## ⚠️ 处理协议(强制,逐条执行,禁止简略)\n\n**收到上面的审稿报告后必须按以下顺序处理**:\n\n1. **逐条编号通读**: 把报告里所有问题、修改建议一项一项列出来(R1, R2, R3...),\n   即使有的看起来重复或次要也要单独保留 — 不允许漏任何一条。\n\n2. **每条给出详细修复方案**:\n   - 补实验: 写明实验配置(baseline / 超参 / seed / dataset / metric) + 预估 GPU 时长\n   - 改论文: 写明改哪一节、改写思路、新引用是否需要补\n   - 改 framing / 措辞: 写明原文 + 改写后版本\n   - 写 rebuttal: 写明论点和支撑证据\n   - 拒绝: 写明拒绝理由(reviewer 误读 / 超出 scope / 已有反驳证据)\n\n3. **必须调 `post_to_reviewer` 让 Research Reviewer 补充意见**:\n   把完整审稿报告 + 你的逐条初步方案打包发给 Reviewer, 让 ta 独立审视有没有\n   遗漏、方案可行性、技术细节是否合理。**这一步不可省** — Reviewer 是你的\n   独立审查员, 他对每条审稿意见的判断和你互补。\n\n4. **收到 Reviewer 补充后综合定稿**: 把 reviewer 反馈合并进你的 Action Matrix,\n   再安排 Main Agent 执行(补实验 / 改论文)或向用户确认 venue 变更。\n   **对 CRITICAL/WARNING 级的修复指令, 必须要求 Agent 修改完成后把修后段落 +\n   原始审稿意见发给 Reviewer 做验证 — Reviewer 以审稿人视角判断修复是否充分,\n   返回 PASS 才算完成。** 不验证就标 resolved 是\"修了但没修对\"的根源。\n\n5. **同步 Issue Tracker**: Action Matrix 定稿后, 用 `read_local_file` 读\n   `projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, 把本轮处理的 issue 标\n   `in_progress`、Reviewer 已确认修复的标 `resolved`, 调 `update_review_issues`\n   写回。这是跨轮次的修复追踪 — Continue 模式审稿要靠这个表识别复发问题。\n\n6. **区分写作问题和 evidence 问题**: 对每条 CRITICAL/WARNING 级审稿意见,\n   先判断: \"如果论文写得完美无缺(措辞零瑕疵),这个问题还存在吗?\"\n   - 是 → 这是 evidence 问题, 不能只改论文。必须补实验/补分析/降级 claim\n   - 否 → 这是写作问题, 可以通过改论文修复\n   审稿人说\"not convincing / insufficient evidence / lacks support / confound /\n   reliability / sample size\"时, 基本都是 evidence 问题 — 不要用加 hedging 应付。\n\n7. **检查复发问题**: 在制定修复方案之前, 先 `read_local_file` 读\n   `projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, 找出本轮审稿意见中\n   哪些是之前已经出现过的老问题。对复发问题重点关注:\n   - 查看上轮用了什么方法修复(action_items 中记录了)\n   - 如果上轮已经用写作手段修复过但审稿人仍然 flag → 写作手段对这个问题无效,\n     必须换路径(补实验 / 补分析 / 降级 claim 位置)\n   - 在 Action Matrix 中标注 \"[复发 × N 轮]\", 并说明为什么这次的修复方案\n     和之前不同\n\n8. **claim 校准是双向的**: 实验结果超预期时 claim 可以升级(扩大 scope / 去掉\n   hedging / 提升到 headline), 实验结果不及预期时 claim 应该降级。降级 claim\n   不是降级论文 — 把一个 evidence 不足的 finding 从 headline 移到 discussion,\n   反而让论文整体更可信。\n\n9. **向 A 群汇报**: Action Matrix 定稿后,用 `director_reply_to_user` 向用户汇报:\n   - 审稿整体评估(系统性问题有哪些、严重程度)\n   - Action Matrix 摘要(补哪些实验、改哪些章节、写哪些 rebuttal)\n   - 预估时间线\n   - 需要用户决策的事项(如有)\n\n**禁止**:\n- 跳过第 3 步直接派 Main Agent\n- 把多条审稿意见合并成一条 action\n- 对低 priority 意见用\"暂不处理\"一笔带过而不给出明确理由\n- 整体性\"已知悉\"回复\n- 对 evidence 问题只用写作手段修复(第 6 条)\n- 对复发问题重复使用已失败的修复方法(第 7 条)\n"
}
```

[审稿报告]
Overall: 3.5 (Borderline Conference)

# Round 20 审稿报告 — Temporal Score Incomparability in LLM Benchmarks

**论文**: Temporal Score Incomparability in LLM Benchmarks: A Large-Scale Psychometric Audit of 5,227 Models
**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 (ARR May cycle)
**Reviewer 配置**: 2 人（R1: 方法严谨性+实验有效性; R2: 新颖性/定位+完整性）
**审稿轮次**: Round 1（1 轮收敛，无需第 2 轮）

---

## Summary

本文将教育测量学中的 Mantel-Haenszel 差异项目功能（MH-DIF）分析引入 LLM 基准测试的时序可比性检验，基于 METABENCH 响应矩阵（5,227 模型 × 12,508 MMLU 题目）发现约 29–31% 的题目在 2023 与 2024 模型群组间呈现大效应 DIF（ETS C 类），且该信号与 web-overlap 污染标签低相关（φ = 0.012）。论文开发了 cross-subject matching 半合成验证框架（ΔFPR = 0 构造性保证），在六个基准上复现了发现（17.9–31.3%），并提出 Monitor/Triage/Correct 审计协议。

---

## Strengths

**S1. 问题定位精准且及时。** [R1-S1, R2-S1] 将 measurement invariance 引入 LLM 评估填补了重要方法论空白。5,227 模型 × 12,508 题目 × 6 基准的规模在同类工作中罕见，核心发现在所有基准上复现（Table 2: C% 17.9–31.3%）。

**S2. Cross-subject matching 设计巧妙。** [R1-S3, R2-S3] 将 purified subtest matching 适配到 LLM 多学科场景，ΔFPR = 0 的构造性保证（Section 3.4 Remark）是方法论亮点。Figure 5 清晰展示 FPR 控制差异。

**S3. 多层次控制实验设计严谨。** [R1-S4, R2-S2] 五种 cohort 对比、DEFF 校正、cluster bootstrap、LOFO 家族分析、random-split baseline（0.62% vs 31.3% = 50×）、IRT θ-conditioning（r = 0.9986）从多角度排除混淆。

**S4. 概念框架有教育意义。** [R2-S4] Figure 2 通过 ICC 曲线清晰展示 global exposure vs differential functioning 区别，对 NLP 评估社区有启发价值。

**S5. Base-vs-instruct 方向反转是有价值的发现。** [R2-S6] χ² = 562.7 的结构化方向性差异超越简单二分法，指向训练方法论对测量特性的系统性影响。

**S6. 心理测量概念解释充分。** [R2-S5, R1-S6] Section 3.2 对 MH/ETS/DIF 概念的铺垫和 Figure 1 pipeline 图对 NLP 读者友好。

---

## Weaknesses

### Must-Fix（影响接收的关键问题）

**W1. [Critical, Consensus] 标题 "Score Incomparability" 与核心发现 ρ ≈ 0.997 存在根本性叙事张力。**
标题暗示分数不可比较，但核心实证发现恰恰是排名几乎完美保持（Table 2: ρ ≈ 0.997, flip rate ≤ 2.9%）。Introduction "The result is sobering" 放大了此不匹配。论文实际展示的是 item-level instability with aggregate robustness——31% 的题目有大效应 DIF，但聚合排名几乎不变。虽然个别模型可移动 ±561 位（Appendix），DIF-ordered removal 比 random removal 更快降低 ρ（Figure 6），但 aggregate 层面的近乎完美保持使 "incomparability" 一词明显过度。
- R1: AGREE | R2: 原始提出
- **跨轮状态**: 新发现（R20 首次提出）
- **建议**: 标题改为更准确的措辞（如 "Temporal Item-Level Instability" 或 "Differential Item Functioning"）；弱化 "sobering" 等措辞

**W2. [Critical, Consensus] 核心 C% 数字的不确定性范围过大（13.5%–31.3%），且 Abstract 使用未校正值。**
Abstract 使用 "31%" 作为 headline claim，但 DEFF 校正后 simple-mean = 29.2%、weighted-mean = 13.5%，2.3× 范围（Section 4.2, Appendix D Table 10）。Cluster bootstrap CI [26.7%, 53.2%] 排除 weighted-mean 的 13.5%，但 bootstrap 依赖于 family 定义的合理性（Appendix I: 30 families with ≥2 members），论文未提供 family 定义的敏感性分析。如果真实 C% 接近 13.5%，"50× above random" 变为 ~22×，叙事力度显著减弱。
- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE
- **跨轮状态**: R19-N1 再次出现。R19→R20 action（改用 29.2% + CI）标记 done，但 Abstract 仍使用 raw "31%"
- **建议**: Abstract 使用 DEFF 校正后数字（~29% 或报告区间）；提供 family 定义敏感性分析收窄区间

**W3. [Critical, Divergence] 方法论新颖性有限——核心方法均为已知技术的直接应用/适配。**
MH-DIF (Holland & Thayer, 1988)、ETS A/B/C 分类、cross-subject matching (purified subtest matching, Swaminathan & Rogers, 1990)、DEFF 校正 (Rao & Scott, 1992) 均非原创。论文的四个 LLM-specific challenges（Section 3.2）的解决方案也是现有方法（permutation simulation、DEFF、quintile stratification、cross-subject matching）。
- R1: 部分 REBUT——cross-subject matching 适配、semi-synthetic injection framework、四个 LLM-specific challenges 的系统性解决是 non-trivial 的设计创新；对 EMNLP 而言，成熟方法严谨适配到新领域并通过大规模实证验证本身有价值
- R2: 原始提出——NLP 顶会对方法论创新要求高于"已知方法到新领域的迁移"
- **跨轮状态**: R19-D1 分歧持续
- **建议**: Introduction 更清晰区分 borrowed methodology 与 novel adaptations；标注哪些是已知方法、哪些是本文的适配创新

### Should-Fix（显著影响论文质量）

**W4. [Major, Consensus] "Mechanism-agnostic" 定位与 contamination-centric 验证/叙事存在张力。**
论文声称 DIF 是 mechanism-agnostic（Section 5.1: "Temporal DIF detects *that* items function differently...not *why*"），但：(1) 验证框架完全基于 contamination injection（Section 3.4）；(2) Section 4.3 大量对比 web-overlap labels；(3) Discussion（Section 5.2）建议 contamination-motivated item removal。如果方法真正 mechanism-agnostic，至少应包含一个非 contamination 来源的 DIF 验证（如模拟训练方法变化）。
- R1: AGREE | R2: 原始提出
- **跨轮状态**: R19-W3 再次出现。Related Work 重排已完成，但验证框架仍单一
- **建议**: 补充至少一个非 contamination 来源的 DIF 验证

**W5. [Major, Consensus] Semi-synthetic 注入使用 uniform binary flipping，可能高估检测力。**
论文在 Limitations (1) 和 Section 3.4 已承认此局限（"reported ΔTPR values may overestimate detection power"），Section 4.4 的 difficulty-dependent injection 量化了 ~20% 高估（ΔTPR ≈ 0.41 vs 0.51）。但验证框架仅覆盖 contamination 注入的不同变体，未覆盖其他 DIF 机制。
- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE（指出论文已有部分回应，~20% bound）
- **跨轮状态**: R19-N2 再次出现。Reframe as calibration upper bound 已完成
- **建议**: 考虑补充 graded injection 实验

**W6. [Major, Consensus] ETS 阈值从人类考生迁移到 LLM 缺乏 substantive meaning 讨论。**
ETS A/B/C 阈值校准于人类教育测试群体。LLM 无真正 latent ability trait；within-family 高同质性 inflate sample size 但不增独立信息。跨阈值鲁棒性分析（Section 4.2: 18.4% at |Δ_MH| ≥ 2.0, 10.2% at ≥ 2.5）部分缓解阈值选择的任意性，但论文未讨论 |Δ_MH| = 1.5 对 LLM 的实质含义，也未提供 LLM-specific 阈值校准。
- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE
- **跨轮状态**: R19-W4 再次出现。已连接 Monte Carlo power + random-split FPR，但 reviewer 要求更深入
- **建议**: 添加 ETS 阈值在 LLM 场景下实质含义的讨论段落

**W7. [Major, Divergence] DIF 在 LLM 场景下的概念合法性讨论不足。**
2023 vs 2024 模型不是同一总体的人口学子群，而是技术上根本不同的系统。κ = 0.80（cross-subject vs within-subject matching agreement）意味着 20% 的 DIF 项目可能是多维性伪影。需讨论：何时 LLM temporal DIF 反映测量问题（item 有问题）vs 合法能力结构变化（model 本来就不同）。
- R1: 部分 REBUT——DIF 对跨时间群体也有效（教育测量先例），Q3 diagnostic + cross-subject matching 部分缓解多维性
- R2: 原始提出
- **建议**: 补充讨论 LLM temporal DIF 的概念边界

**W8. [Major, Consensus] Base-vs-instruct 反向模式（χ² = 562.7）机制解释不足。**
RLHF/instruction tuning 系统性地改变模型与 MCQ 的交互方式（format preference, safety behavior, calibration），这些都可能产生 DIF-like artifacts 而非真正的 item-level bias。论文在 Section 5.1 将反转解释为 "structured item-level bias rather than uniform group impact"，但未排除上述 confound。Appendix O (Table 15) 的 base-only/instruct-only temporal DIF 分析应被提升到主文以回应此质疑。
- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE
- **跨轮状态**: 新发现（R20 首次提出）
- **建议**: 讨论 format sensitivity 作为替代解释；将 Table 15 提升到主文

**W9. [Major, Partial Consensus] Cross-subject ΔFPR = 0 保证在 k = 5–15 区间退化未充分表征。**
k ≤ 5 ΔFPR < 0.007，k = 20 ΔFPR = 0.167。中间区域缺乏系统刻画，对 practitioner 判断何时切换 within-subject matching 至关重要。
- R1: 原始提出 | R2: 部分 AGREE（"加分项"而非"否决性"缺陷）
- **跨轮状态**: R19-W2 再次出现
- **建议**: 补充 k = 5, 8, 10, 15 的 ΔFPR 数据点

**W10. [Moderate, Consensus] "近独立于 web-overlap labels" 的推断强度过高。**
φ = 0.012 可能反映 web-overlap labels 的 proxy 噪声而非 DIF 的真正独立性。论文已承认此可能性（Section 4.3），并提供 label-independent 支持证据（base-vs-instruct reversal），但 "complementary diagnostic dimensions" 措辞仍过强。
- R1: 原始提出 | R2: AGREE
- **建议**: 弱化措辞为 "low correlation with available contamination proxies"

**W11. [Minor, Consensus] Contribution 3 (audit protocol) 权重不足。**
Section 5.2 仅半段，无部署验证、case study 或用户研究。作为声明的三大贡献之一显得单薄。
- R1: AGREE | R2: 原始提出
- **建议**: 降级为 future direction，或扩展并提供模拟部署场景

---

## Reviewer Consensus

[AGREE] 双方一致同意的关键问题（7 项）：
1. 标题 "Score Incomparability" 与 ρ ≈ 0.997 的叙事不匹配 (W1)
2. C% 不确定性和 Abstract headline claim 表述 (W2)
3. Mechanism-agnostic 定位与 contamination 验证的张力 (W4)
4. Base-vs-instruct 反转的机制解释不足 (W8)
5. ETS 阈值迁移的实质含义讨论不足 (W6)
6. Web-overlap labels 推断强度过高 (W10)
7. Contribution 3 权重不足 (W11)

## Reviewer Divergence

[REBUT/Partial] 存在分歧的问题（4 项）：

1. **方法论新颖性** (W3): R2 认为核心方法为直接搬运（Critical），R1 反驳 cross-subject matching 适配和 semi-synthetic framework 是 non-trivial 的设计创新。分歧未收敛。

2. **2PL power analysis vs IRT misfit** (R1-W3): R2 反驳——MH-DIF 是非参数方法，power analysis 是辅助证据，实证 50× ratio 是核心。R1 接受但建议论文更明确讨论 2PL misfit 影响。**降级为 minor concern。**

3. **多重比较校正** (R1-W6): R2 反驳——DIF 领域标准实践不做 FWER/FDR correction（Wainer 1993），BH 校正仅影响 3/3918 C items (0.08%)。**降级为 minor concern。**

4. **DIF 概念合法性** (W7): R1 部分反驳——DIF 对跨时间群体有效（教育测量先例），Q3 diagnostic 部分缓解多维性。但同意需更多讨论。

---

## Questions

1. Abstract 是否应使用 DEFF 校正后数字（~29% + CI）而非 raw 31%？为何选择 simple-mean 而非 weighted-mean DEFF？
2. 能否提供 family 定义的敏感性分析以收窄 C% 区间？
3. 既然 ρ ≈ 0.997，temporal DIF 的 practical impact 是什么？在什么具体场景下 practitioner 会因忽略 temporal DIF 做出错误决策？
4. DIF 信号是线性积累还是存在突变点？Rolling-window MH-DIF 分析结果如何？
5. Base-only / instruct-only temporal DIF (Table 15) 为何不在主文展示？
6. 如果补充非 contamination 来源的 DIF 验证（如训练方法变化模拟），结果如何？
7. |Δ_MH| = 1.5 对 LLM 的实质含义是什么？能否提供 LLM-specific 阈值校准？
8. Cross-subject matching 在全局训练方法变化驱动 DIF 时的行为如何？（R1 论证为 conservative bias，不导致 false positive inflation，但可能 under-powered）

---

## Missing References

1. **Rodriguez et al. (2021) "Evaluation Examples Are Not Equally Informative" (ACL 2021)** — IRT+NLP 评估的奠基性工作 [R1 AGREE, R2 提出]
2. **Polo et al. (2024) "tinyBenchmarks" (ICML 2024)** — IRT-based 高效 LLM 评估 [R2 提出; R1 部分 REBUT: 技术路线差异大]
3. **Vania et al. (2021) "Comparing Test Sets with Item Response Theory"** — NLP+IRT 早期工作 [R1 提出]
4. **van der Linden & Veldkamp (2007)** — Item exposure / test security 文献 [R1 提出; R2: nice-to-have]

---

## External Review (Stanford Agentic Reviewer)

外部审稿：未配置，不适用。

---

## Visual Layout Check（非评分项）

视觉审查已完成。7 个 info 级别发现，0 critical，0 warning：

- **Page 2**: Figure 1 response matrix 彩色小点灰度打印辨识度不足，建议辅以 marker shape 或 hatching
- **Page 4**: Figure 3 ETS 颜色编码（A=绿, B=黄, C=红）对红绿色盲不友好，建议改用 Okabe-Ito 色板或 blue-orange 方案
- **Page 5**: Figure 4 x 轴标签字号偏小，部分重叠，建议调整间距或旋转
- **Page 7**: Figure 6 五个面板 tick/legend 文字偏小（约正文 60-65%），建议增大字号
- **Page 8**: 右栏内容密度极高（§4.5–4.7 + §5 + §6），无 display item 穿插，但作为 Discussion/Conclusion 区域属正常安排
- **Page 13**: Tables 6–8 密集排布，单个表格行数少（3-5 行），可考虑合并
- **Page 14**: Table 10 列头缩写较多，建议 caption 解释

---

## Overall Assessment

**Overall Score: 3.5 (Borderline Conference)**

| 维度 | R1 | R2 | 综合 |
|------|----|----|------|
| Soundness | 3/5 | 4/5 | 3.5/5 |
| Excitement | 3.5/5 | 3/5 | 3.0/5 |
| Reproducibility | 4/5 | — | 4/5 |
| Confidence | 4/5 | 4/5 | 4/5 |

**综合评判**: 这是一篇执行质量高的大规模实证论文，问题重要且及时，多层次验证设计严谨，cross-subject matching 的 ΔFPR = 0 保证有方法论价值。但存在三个核心障碍阻碍其升至 Conference (4.0)：

1. **叙事过度**: 标题 "Score Incomparability" 与 ρ ≈ 0.997 的发现直接矛盾；Abstract 使用未校正的 31% 而非 DEFF 校正后的区间
2. **方法论新颖性争议**: 核心统计方法均为 30+ 年历史的标准工具，LLM-specific 适配的创新程度在两位 reviewer 间有分歧
3. **验证框架单一**: 声称 mechanism-agnostic 但验证完全基于 contamination injection

**升至 Conference (4.0) 的路径** (rebuttal 可解决)：
- (a) 修改标题和 Abstract，使用 DEFF 校正后数字，诚实反映不确定性
- (b) 在 Introduction 中明确区分 borrowed methodology 与 novel adaptations
- (c) 补充至少一个非 contamination DIF 机制的验证
- (d) 讨论 ETS 阈值在 LLM 场景下的 substantive meaning
- (e) 提供 family 定义敏感性分析以收窄 C% 区间
- (f) 将 base-only/instruct-only temporal DIF (Table 15) 提升到主文

**作为 Findings 论文** (3.0–3.5): 论文完全达标——规模大、执行扎实、对社区有诊断价值。

---

## Detailed Comments

**Abstract**: 信息密度适中（~150 words，符合 ≤200 words 要求），但 "31%" headline 未反映 DEFF 校正后的不确定性。"near-independent" 措辞过强。

**Introduction (Section 1)**: Figure 1 pipeline 图信息量大，四步管线清晰。三个 contributions 权重不均：C3 (audit protocol) 后续 Section 5.2 仅半段，缺乏部署验证。"The result is sobering" 与 ρ ≈ 0.997 的 aggregate robustness 不一致。

**Related Work (Section 2)**: 组织合理（Psychometric Methods → Contamination Detection → Benchmark Quality）。与 Growing Pains 的区分清晰（nonparametric vs parametric, DIF diagnosis vs score equating）。遗漏 Rodriguez et al. (2021)。

**Method (Section 3)**: MH-DIF 和 ETS 分类的解释清晰，包括公式推导（Eq. 1-2）和四个 LLM-specific challenges 的讨论。Cross-subject matching 的 Remark 论证有说服力。Semi-synthetic injection 的 uniform flipping 局限性在 Limitations 中承认，但 Section 3.4 正文措辞不够 cautious——应在首次提出时标注 "calibration upper bound"。

**Experiments (Section 4)**: 多层次控制设计严谨。Section 4.2 的 DEFF 区间处理（"bracketing C% at 13.5–29.2%"）不够 transparent——应在主文而非仅附录讨论 estimator 选择。Section 4.3 的 web-overlap 分析有 proxy noise confound。Section 4.2 "DIF rates are domain-invariant (KW p = 0.78)" 与 "DIF direction exhibits domain dependence" 的并置需更清晰铺垫。

**Discussion (Section 5)**: Section 5.1 的 base-vs-instruct 分析有价值但机制解释不足——需讨论 format sensitivity 作为替代解释。Section 5.2 audit protocol 过于简短。

**Conclusion (Section 6)**: 单句 conclusion 信息量可接受但给人仓促收尾之感。

**Limitations**: 覆盖 3 个关键局限（injection realism, non-independence, cross-subject matching boundary），符合 EMNLP 要求。

**Appendix**: A–Q (17 个) 内容详尽，但数量较多，可考虑合并部分相关性强的 appendix。

**Format**: 正文 8 页内符合限制；Limitations 在第 9 页不计入页数；匿名链接 anonymous.4open.science 格式正确；引用格式符合 ACL 规范。
---

## ⚠️ 处理协议(强制,逐条执行,禁止简略)

**收到上面的审稿报告后必须按以下顺序处理**:

1. **逐条编号通读**: 把报告里所有问题、修改建议一项一项列出来(R1, R2, R3...),
   即使有的看起来重复或次要也要单独保留 — 不允许漏任何一条。

2. **每条给出详细修复方案**:
   - 补实验: 写明实验配置(baseline / 超参 / seed / dataset / metric) + 预估 GPU 时长
   - 改论文: 写明改哪一节、改写思路、新引用是否需要补
   - 改 framing / 措辞: 写明原文 + 改写后版本
   - 写 rebuttal: 写明论点和支撑证据
   - 拒绝: 写明拒绝理由(reviewer 误读 / 超出 scope / 已有反驳证据)

3. **必须调 `post_to_reviewer` 让 Research Reviewer 补充意见**:
   把完整审稿报告 + 你的逐条初步方案打包发给 Reviewer, 让 ta 独立审视有没有
   遗漏、方案可行性、技术细节是否合理。**这一步不可省** — Reviewer 是你的
   独立审查员, 他对每条审稿意见的判断和你互补。

4. **收到 Reviewer 补充后综合定稿**: 把 reviewer 反馈合并进你的 Action Matrix,
   再安排 Main Agent 执行(补实验 / 改论文)或向用户确认 venue 变更。
   **对 CRITICAL/WARNING 级的修复指令, 必须要求 Agent 修改完成后把修后段落 +
   原始审稿意见发给 Reviewer 做验证 — Reviewer 以审稿人视角判断修复是否充分,
   返回 PASS 才算完成。** 不验证就标 resolved 是"修了但没修对"的根源。

5. **同步 Issue Tracker**: Action Matrix 定稿后, 用 `read_local_file` 读
   `projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, 把本轮处理的 issue 标
   `in_progress`、Reviewer 已确认修复的标 `resolved`, 调 `update_review_issues`
   写回。这是跨轮次的修复追踪 — Continue 模式审稿要靠这个表识别复发问题。

6. **区分写作问题和 evidence 问题**: 对每条 CRITICAL/WARNING 级审稿意见,
   先判断: "如果论文写得完美无缺(措辞零瑕疵),这个问题还存在吗?"
   - 是 → 这是 evidence 问题, 不能只改论文。必须补实验/补分析/降级 claim
   - 否 → 这是写作问题, 可以通过改论文修复
   审稿人说"not convincing / insufficient evidence / lacks support / confound /
   reliability / sample size"时, 基本都是 evidence 问题 — 不要用加 hedging 应付。

7. **检查复发问题**: 在制定修复方案之前, 先 `read_local_file` 读
   `projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, 找出本轮审稿意见中
   哪些是之前已经出现过的老问题。对复发问题重点关注:
   - 查看上轮用了什么方法修复(action_items 中记录了)
   - 如果上轮已经用写作手段修复过但审稿人仍然 flag → 写作手段对这个问题无效,
     必须换路径(补实验 / 补分析 / 降级 claim 位置)
   - 在 Action Matrix 中标注 "[复发 × N 轮]", 并说明为什么这次的修复方案
     和之前不同

8. **claim 校准是双向的**: 实验结果超预期时 claim 可以升级(扩大 scope / 去掉
   hedging / 提升到 headline), 实验结果不及预期时 claim 应该降级。降级 claim
   不是降级论文 — 把一个 evidence 不足的 finding 从 headline 移到 discussion,
   反而让论文整体更可信。

9. **向 A 群汇报**: Action Matrix 定稿后,用 `director_reply_to_user` 向用户汇报:
   - 审稿整体评估(系统性问题有哪些、严重程度)
   - Action Matrix 摘要(补哪些实验、改哪些章节、写哪些 rebuttal)
   - 预估时间线
   - 需要用户决策的事项(如有)

**禁止**:
- 跳过第 3 步直接派 Main Agent
- 把多条审稿意见合并成一条 action
- 对低 priority 意见用"暂不处理"一笔带过而不给出明确理由
- 整体性"已知悉"回复
- 对 evidence 问题只用写作手段修复(第 6 条)
- 对复发问题重复使用已失败的修复方法(第 7 条)

No Ethical Considerations section exists. Let me check if the work needs one. The paper deals with:

This is primarily a measurement and evaluation methodology paper without human subjects, crowdsourcing, or models that could be misused. While it does involve a large dataset of LLM evaluations and discusses contamination, the work relies on public benchmarks and public leaderboard data, so there aren't significant data privacy concerns. Since the paper doesn't involve any of the key areas that would warrant an ethical considerations section, I don't think one is necessary here. doesn't involve human subjects or proprietary data, so I don't think an ethics section is necessary here. ```text
1	\section{Method Details}
2	\label{app:method_details}
3	
4	\subsection{Diagnostics}
5	\label{app:diagnostics}
6	
7	Three diagnostic procedures complement the primary DIF analysis.
8	
9	\paragraph{Local independence (Yen's Q3).} The MH procedure assumes that item responses are conditionally independent given $\theta$. Yen's Q3 statistic measures residual correlations between item pairs after conditioning on the latent trait. We compute Q3 for all within-subject and cross-subject item pairs, with $|Q3| > 0.20$ as the threshold for local dependence. Elevated within-subject dependence would indicate that items within the same subject share variance beyond what $\theta$ captures, which could inflate within-subject DIF rates.
10	
11	\paragraph{Domain-specificity.} We test whether temporal DIF concentrates in domains where models improved most. At the ecological level, we compute Spearman $\rho$ between subject-level C\% and subject-level accuracy improvement from 2023 to 2024. At the item level, logistic regression predicts item-level DIF~C classification from domain-level accuracy change, with and without item property controls (difficulty, discrimination). This decomposition quantifies how much of the observed instability is attributable to aggregate domain-level gains versus item-specific behavioral change.
12	
13	\paragraph{Instability decomposition.} When domain accuracy improvement is included alongside item-level controls, we compute the counterfactual C\% that would remain after removing domain-level effects. The gap between observed and counterfactual C\% measures the contribution of domain-level improvement to the total instability.
14	
15	\subsection{Statistical Considerations}
16	\label{app:statistical}
17	
18	\paragraph{Multiple testing.} The ETS~C classification imposes a dual threshold (effect size $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ and significance $p < 0.05$), which provides implicit control over false positives. As a robustness check, we apply Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction at $q = 0.05$. The random-split sanity check provides an empirical estimate of the false positive rate under the null hypothesis.
19	
20	\paragraph{Threshold sensitivity.} We report results across a range of DIF thresholds ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \in \{1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5\}$) to verify that qualitative conclusions do not depend on the specific ETS~C cutoff. The standard threshold of $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ corresponds to the ETS~C definition used throughout the paper.
21	
22	\paragraph{IRT model fit.} For transparency, we report that the MMLU response matrix shows poor fit to a unidimensional 2PL model (expected for a 57-subject multidimensional benchmark). This does not affect MH-DIF validity, which relies only on total score stratification rather than parametric IRT assumptions.
23	
24	\paragraph{Architecture heterogeneity.} The METABENCH MMLU population consists of 99.98\% decoder-only models (5,226 of 5,227). This near-complete architectural homogeneity eliminates architecture as a confound for DIF analysis. We verify this by excluding the single encoder-decoder model and confirming identical results.
25	
26	\paragraph{Power sensitivity to item discrimination.}
27	\label{app:power_sensitivity}
28	The Monte Carlo power analysis in \Cref{sec:validation} uses average 2PL parameters ($\bar{a} = 1.2$, $\bar{b} = -0.84$). \Cref{tab:power_sensitivity} reports TPR as a function of discrimination $a$ (other parameters at their means, $N = 80$ per cohort, $|\Delta_b| = 1.4$, 1,000 replications). TPR increases monotonically from 0.40 ($a = 0.5$) to 0.95 ($a = 2.0$); FPR remains below 0.04 throughout. The MMLU item pool shows substantial heterogeneity: $a$ ranges from 0.006 to 2.657 (SD $= 0.54$, IQR $= [0.75, 1.64]$). The aggregate TPR of 0.68 from Phase~2 reflects this full distribution, while TPR at mean $a \approx 1.2$ is 0.87; the gap is consistent with Jensen's inequality applied to the concave TPR($a$) function. Although 2PL fit is poor for MMLU's multidimensional structure, the power analysis serves as one leg of a triangulation: parametric estimates (TPR $= 0.68$--$0.80$) are validated by empirical semi-synthetic injection ($\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = {+}0.508$ at $p = 0.5$; \Cref{sec:semisynthetic}), confirming adequate detection power regardless of parametric assumptions.
29	
30	\begin{table}[h]
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01BfbbPB3vEemKwMwdNGiW3g`</summary>

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	(1)~The semi-synthetic injection framework uses uniform binary response flipping; realistic contamination (partial memorization, format-specific advantages) would produce weaker DIF signals, so reported $\Delta\mathrm{TPR}$ values may overestimate detection power. The framework detects \emph{that} items function differently but not \emph{why}. (2)~The MH $\chi^2$ test assumes independent examinees; LLM model families violate this assumption. DEFF correction and cluster bootstrap bound the impact, but extreme within-family relatedness (e.g., hundreds of Llama-2 fine-tunes) exceeds educational-testing precedents~\citep{french2010hierarchical}. (3)~Cross-subject matching assumes contamination affects a minority of domains ($\Delta\mathrm{FPR}$ rises when contamination spans ${\geq}20$ subjects), and generalization beyond MCQ benchmarks requires further validation.
5	
```

392	The 2023 and 2024 cohorts differ in base/instruct composition (2023: 79.7\% base; 2024: 67.9\% base; 11.8\,pp gap). To test whether this confounds temporal DIF, we downsample 2023 base models to match the 2024 ratio and re-run MH-DIF (10 random seeds). Balanced C\% $= 30.25\% \pm 1.32\%$ (95\% CI: $[27.65\%, 32.84\%]$), differing from the original 31.3\% by only 1.08\,pp. Composition shift does not explain the observed temporal DIF rate.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PART 3 — Responsible Research Checklist
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Rules (from ARR official policy):
  - Yes → MUST provide section number + English elaboration sentence
  - No  → MUST provide 1-2 sentence English justification
          (NOT acceptable: "Not mentioned.", "未涉及")
  - N/A → Brief English explanation (e.g., "No human annotators used.")

──────────────────────────────────
A. Limitations
──────────────────────────────────

▸ A1 Limitations Section*
  Authors are required to discuss the limitations of their work in a dedicated
  section titled "Limitations" (not counting towards page limit). Papers without
  this section will be desk rejected.
  → Must be Yes. Section: §?

▸ A2 Potential Risks*
  Did you discuss any potential risks of your work?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → A2 Elaboration (English): ?

──────────────────────────────────
B. Use Or Create Scientific Artifacts
──────────────────────────────────

▸ B Use Or Create Scientific Artifacts*
  Did you use or create scientific artifacts? (e.g. code, datasets, models)
  → Yes / No

▸ B1 Cite Creators Of Artifacts*
  Did you cite the creators of artifacts you used?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → B1 Elaboration: ?

▸ B2 Discuss The License For Artifacts*
  Did you discuss the license or terms for use and/or distribution of any
  artifacts?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → B2 Elaboration: ?

▸ B3 Artifact Use Consistent With Intended Use*
  Did you discuss if your use of existing artifact(s) was consistent with their
  intended use? For artifacts you create, do you specify intended use?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → B3 Elaboration: ?

▸ B4 Data Contains Personally Identifying Info Or Offensive Content*
  Did you discuss the steps taken to check whether the data contains PII or
  offensive content, and steps taken to protect/anonymize it?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → B4 Elaboration: ?

▸ B5 Documentation Of Artifacts*
  Did you provide documentation of the artifacts (coverage of domains, languages,
  linguistic phenomena, demographic groups, etc.)?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → B5 Elaboration: ?

▸ B6 Statistics For Data*
  Did you report relevant statistics like the number of examples, details of
  train/test/dev splits, etc.?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → B6 Elaboration: ?

──────────────────────────────────
C. Computational Experiments
──────────────────────────────────

▸ C Computational Experiments*
  Did you run computational experiments?
  → Yes / No

▸ C1 Model Size And Budget*
  Did you report the number of parameters in the models used, the total
  computational budget (e.g., GPU hours), and computing infrastructure used?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → C1 Elaboration: ?

▸ C2 Experimental Setup And Hyperparameters*
  Did you discuss the experimental setup, including hyperparameter search and
  best-found hyperparameter values?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → C2 Elaboration: ?

▸ C3 Descriptive Statistics*
  Did you report descriptive statistics about your results (e.g., error bars,
  summary statistics), and is it transparent whether you are reporting the max,
  mean, etc. or just a single run?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → C3 Elaboration: ?

▸ C4 Parameters For Packages*
  If you used existing packages (e.g., NLTK, SpaCy, ROUGE), did you report the
  implementation, model, and parameter settings used?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → C4 Elaboration: ?

──────────────────────────────────
D. Human Subjects Including Annotators
──────────────────────────────────

▸ D Human Subjects Including Annotators*
  Did you use human annotators (e.g., crowdworkers) or research with human
  subjects?
  → Yes / No

▸ D1 Instructions Given To Participants*
  Did you report the full text of instructions given to participants, including
  screenshots, disclaimers of any risks, etc.?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → D1 Elaboration: ?

▸ D2 Recruitment And Payment*
  Did you report information about how you recruited and paid participants, and
  discuss if such payment is adequate?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → D2 Elaboration: ?

▸ D3 Data Consent*
  Did you discuss whether and how consent was obtained from people whose data
  you're using/curating?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → D3 Elaboration: ?

▸ D4 Ethics Review Board Approval*
  Was the data collection protocol approved (or determined exempt) by an ethics
  review board?
  → Yes / No / N/A
  → D4 Elaboration: ?

──────────────────────────────────
E. AI Assistants In Research Or Writing
──────────────────────────────────

▸ E AI Assistants In Research Or Writing*
  Did you use AI assistants (e.g., ChatGPT, Copilot) in your research, coding,
  or writing?
  → Must be Yes (already disclosed in your paper)

▸ E1 Information About Use Of AI Assistants*
  If you used AI assistants, did you include information about their use?
  → Yes
  → E1 Elaboration: Point to the "Use of AI Assistance" section in your
    Appendix. Example:
    "Appendix X — Claude used for coding, code drafting, debugging, and language polishing."

392	The 2023 and 2024 cohorts differ in base/instruct composition (2023: 79.7\% base; 2024: 67.9\% base; 11.8\,pp gap). Balanced C\% $= 30.25\% \pm 1.32\%$ (95\% CI: $[27.65\%, 32.84\%]$), differing from the original 31.3\% by only 1.08\,pp. Composition shift does not explain the observed temporal DIF rate.

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Every leaderboard comparison assumes that benchmark items measure the same construct across model generations. In educational testing, this foundational property, measurement invariance, is routinely verified. In large language model (LLM) evaluation, it has never been tested at scale. We bring differential item functioning (DIF) analysis from psychometrics to the METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models on MMLU) and find that 31\% of items function systematically differently between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts, 50$\times$ above random baselines. This instability is not driven by data contamination: DIF flags are near-independent of web-overlap labels ($\phi = 0.012$), an overlooked diagnostic dimension. Nor does it threaten validity: aggregate rankings remain near-perfect ($\rho \approx 0.997$). Instead, unstable items expose which capability dimensions shift across generations; base and instruct models exhibit opposite DIF directions. We develop a semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching that guarantees zero false-positive inflation, replicate findings across six benchmarks (18--31\% instability), and propose an actionable audit protocol. LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring, not just score reporting\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/irt_equated_bench_regen-2431/}.}.
3	\end{abstract}
4	
```

For keywords, I'm thinking about what would best represent this work for the Resources and Evaluation track—terms like measurement invariance, differential item functioning, benchmark stability, and LLM evaluation methodology seem most relevant to capture both the psychometric approach and the evaluation focus.

  "text": "**ARR/EMNLP OpenReview Submission Form — Completed**\n\n══════════════════════════════════════\n【PART 0】\n══════════════════════════════════════\n\n**Title:** Temporal Score Incomparability in LLM Benchmarks: A Large-Scale Psychometric Audit of 5,227 Models\n\n**Abstract:**\n\"\"\"\nEvery leaderboard comparison assumes that benchmark items measure the same construct across model generations. In educational testing, this foundational property, measurement invariance, is routinely verified. In large language model (LLM) evaluation, it has never been tested at scale. We bring differential item functioning (DIF) analysis from psychometrics to the METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models on MMLU) and find that 31% of items function systematically differently between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts, 50x above random baselines. This instability is not driven by data contamination: DIF flags are near-independent of web-overlap labels (phi = 0.012), an overlooked diagnostic dimension. Nor does it threaten validity: aggregate rankings remain near-perfect (rho ~ 0.997). Instead, unstable items expose which capability dimensions shift across generations; base and instruct models exhibit opposite DIF directions. We develop a semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching that guarantees zero false-positive inflation, replicate findings across six benchmarks (18-31% instability), and propose an actionable audit protocol. ```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\subsection{Temporal DIF as Structured Signal}
5	\label{sec:disc_temporal}
6	
7	\looseness=-2
8	Temporal DIF detects \emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \emph{why}, by design (\Cref{sec:cohorts}). Stratifying by model type reveals opposite patterns: base models ($N{=}1{,}409$) are 60.1\% reference-favoring ($r = -0.570$), while instruct models ($N{=}478$) are 65.6\% focal-favoring ($\chi^2 = 562.7$, $p < 10^{-124}$). This reversal reflects structured item-level bias rather than uniform group impact~\citep{camilli1994methods}, consistent with DIF as a mechanism-agnostic diagnostic; mixed-mechanism injection cannot match the near-zero instruct gradient, pointing to non-contamination drivers (\Cref{app:mechanism_discussion,app:mixed_mechanism}). Composition downsampling ($\Delta = 1.08$\,pp) and base-only/instruct-only analyses confirm DIF is not a cohort-structure artifact (\Cref{app:composition_control,app:baseinst_temporal,app:within_arch}). Three features distinguish LLM temporal DIF from educational settings: C\% (29.2\%) substantially exceeds typical rates~\citep{clauser1998dif}; DIF flags and web-overlap labels are near-independent (Jaccard $= 0.206$); and the base/instruct reversal (OR $= 4.75$) favors a broader-signal reading over distinct-construct independence.
9	
10	\subsection{Recommended DIF Audit Protocol}
11	\label{sec:disc_protocol}
12	
13	\looseness=-2
14	Item-level properties do not predict DIF susceptibility (AUROC $= 0.60$; \Cref{app:predictive}), precluding pre-screening. We recommend: (1)~\emph{Monitor}: run MH-DIF audits when ${\geq}$500 models accumulate, triggering review when C\% exceeds $20\times$ the random-split baseline; (2)~\emph{Triage}: DIF $\times$ web-overlap co-occurrence warrants removal, DIF on web-clean items warrants monitoring; (3)~\emph{Correct}: expert review, not automatic removal~\citep{camilli1994methods,penfield2007dif} (\Cref{app:score_inflation}).
15

We recommend: (1)~\\emph{Monitor}: run MH-DIF audits when ${\\geq}$500 models accumulate, triggering review when C\\% exceeds $20\\times$ the random-split baseline; (2)~\\emph{Triage}: DIF $\\times$ web-overlap co-occurrence warrants removal, DIF on web-clean items warrants monitoring; (3)~\\emph{Correct}: expert review, not automatic removal~\\citep{camilli1994methods,penfield2007dif} (\\Cref{app:score_inflation}).\n",

    }

_source line 1087 · 2026-05-26T06:39:20.522Z_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 匿名化最后扫雷：日期 + 内部编号

发现有些论文里残留了**具体实验日期**和**内部编号**——这两种东西是 reviewer 一查就能反向定位作者 / 团队的硬证据，必须彻底清掉。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
1. 必须删 / 改：具体日期
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

任何能锁到一周内的时间点都不允许出现。包括但不限于：

❌ "Experiments were conducted on May 18, 2026..."
❌ "We collected data between 2026-04-10 and 2026-04-25..."
❌ "Models were trained on 2026/05/15..."
❌ "Snapshot from 2026-Q1..."
❌ "as of October 2025"
❌ "the recent release of GPT-5 on July 22..."

替换为相对 / 模糊表述：

✅ "Experiments were conducted in recent months."
✅ "Data was collected over a two-week period."
✅ "Models were trained for X GPU-hours."

唯一例外：引用别人论文 / 数据集发布时间（"Llama-3 (Meta, 2024)" 这种是 citation，不算）。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
2. 必须删 / 改：内部编号
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

任何内部追溯编号都不允许出现：

❌ Experiment ID: "exp_2026_0518_a3" / "run_42_v7"
❌ WandB run ID / wandb.ai 链接
❌ 项目内部 codename（如 cost-verify-agent-rl 这种）
❌ Git commit hash
❌ Internal ticket / Jira ID
❌ Server hostname / GPU node 名（"trained on cqa1-33015"）
❌ 内部文件夹路径（"/home/USER/exp_logs/..."）
❌ Orchestration 工具名（Ralph / Director / Manager / Agent 等）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
3. 容易漏检的位置
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

不只检查正文。下列位置最容易残留：

- Figure / Table caption
- Appendix 里的 log snippet / yaml dump / shell output
- Hyperparameter 表
- \texttt{} / \verb{} 代码块
- .tex 注释（% 后面的内容）
- .bib 文件里相关字段
- PDF 元数据

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 各类命中位置数量：日期 X 处，内部编号 Y 处
(b) 每一处的处理方式（改成什么 / 删了什么）
(c) 修完后确认 0 残留

To test whether this confounds temporal DIF, we downsample 2023 base models to match the 2024 ratio and re-run MH-DIF (10 random seeds). Balanced C\% $= 30.25\% \pm 1.32\%$ (95\% CI: $[27.65\%, 32.84\%]$), differing from the original 31.3\% by only 1.08\,pp. Composition shift does not explain the observed temporal DIF rate.

The heavy tails show that 31.3\\% of items exhibit large DIF, 50$\\times$ above random baselines (\\Cref{sec:temporal}).}\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/method.tex:46:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/abstract.tex:2:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:11:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:78:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:81:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:116:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:118:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:325:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:350:\\caption{Within-architecture temporal DIF. C\\% = ETS~C items between 2023 and 2024 models within each lineage.}\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:365:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/appendix.tex:392:The 2023 and 2024 cohorts differ in base/instruct composition (2023: 79.7\\% base; 2024: 67.9\\% base; 11.8\\,pp gap). To test whether this confounds temporal DIF, we downsample 2023 base models to match the 2024 ratio and re-run MH-DIF (10 random seeds). Balanced C\\% $= 30.25\\% \\pm 1.32\\%$ (95\\% CI: $[27.65\\%, 32.84\\%]$), differing from the original 31.3\\% by only 1.08\\,pp. Composition shift does not explain the observed temporal DIF rate.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/introduction.tex:5:[Omitted long matching line]\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/paper/sections/introduction.tex:30:\\item \\textbf{Temporal DIF at scale}: 31.3\\% of MMLU items exhibit large temporal DIF (17.9--31.3\\% across six benchmarks; 29.2\\% after non-independence correction), near-independent of web-overlap labels (Jaccard $= 0.206$); base-vs-instruct stratification reveals opposite patterns ($\\chi^2 = 562.7$), consistent with structured capability shifts rather than uniform exposure (\\Cref{sec:temporal,sec:groundtruth,sec:gsm8k,sec:disc_temporal}).",

```text
Let me check the results:

1. **LaTeX comments**: No `%`-started lines with identifying info found in sections/
2. **PDF metadata**: 
   - Author: empty ✓
   - Title: empty ✓
   - CreationDate/ModDate: "Tue May 26 03:17:53 2026 UTC" — this is the compilation timestamp. This is standard for any PDF and is not identifying info in the same way as content dates. LaTeX always embeds this. However, to be thorough, we could suppress this with `\pdftrailerid{}` and `\pdfsuppressptexinfo=-1` or `\pdfinfo{/CreationDate () /ModDate ()}` but this is extremely unusual for ARR submissions and could actually cause issues. 

The ACL template doesn't suppress these either, so I'll leave it as is since it's standard LaTeX behavior. The abstract uses the correct anonymized link format with `anonymous.4open.science`. Now I need to examine the .bib file and main.tex more carefully for any other identifying content.

{
  "type": "queue-operation",
  "operation": "enqueue",
  "timestamp": "2026-05-26T08:18:33.949Z",
  "sessionId": "78cfbf9f-97b6-46ed-a5de-e1517a0d03f9",
  "content": "[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：前向引用问题（先用后定义）\n\n发现有些论文里出现\"先用后定义\"的情况——某个概念 / 符号 / 模块 / 缩写在 Section X 里直接用了，但它的定义放在 Section X+N 才出现。这是 AI 写作的典型问题，reviewer 读到这里会觉得\"不知所云、组织混乱\"。\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n检查范围\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n每个东西第一次出现的位置，必须**已经**完成定义。包括：\n\n□ 缩写 / 简写（如 RLHF / DPO / IRT，第一次出现要写全称 + 缩写）\n□ 自定义概念 / 术语（你 paper 自己造的词，如 \"behavioral death\"、\"sycophancy score\"）\n□ 数学符号 / 公式中的变量（每个变量在第一次出现的方程里就要解释）\n□ 自定义方法 / 模块组件（\"the consistency gate\"、\"the alignment head\"）\n□ 评测指标（自定义的 metric，必须在 main result 表出现之前定义清楚）\n□ 数据集 / setting 的内部命名（\"the strict split\"、\"the holdout pool\"）\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n常见违规模式\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n❌ Abstract / Intro 里用了一个自造术语，Method 才定义\n❌ Intro 提到 \"we use SYCONScore\"，到 §4 才看到这个 metric 的定义\n❌ Method §2 用了变量 α，到 §2.3 才写 \"where α denotes...\"\n❌ Method 提到 \"module M\"，结构图 + 解释在 Appendix 才有\n❌ Table 1 列了 \"Score@K\"，K 在 §5 才说取什么值\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n修复策略\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n每个违规位置二选一：\n\nA) **把定义提前**到第一次使用之前。最干净。\nB) 在第一次使用的位置**加一个简短的 inline 解释**（一句话 / 括号说明）+ \"(formally defined in §X)\" 引用前向。容忍度更高的妥协方案。\n\n注意：A 优先。只有 A 会改动太多结构时，才退而求其次用 B。\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n汇报内容\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n(a) 找到多少处先用后定义？分布在哪些章节？\n(b) 每一处用 A 还是 B 修的？\n(c) 修完后 PDF 编译通过"
}
```

_source line 1128 · 2026-05-26T08:18:34.147Z_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：前向引用问题（先用后定义）

发现有些论文里出现"先用后定义"的情况——某个概念 / 符号 / 模块 / 缩写在 Section X 里直接用了，但它的定义放在 Section X+N 才出现。这是 AI 写作的典型问题，reviewer 读到这里会觉得"不知所云、组织混乱"。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
检查范围
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

每个东西第一次出现的位置，必须**已经**完成定义。包括：

□ 缩写 / 简写（如 RLHF / DPO / IRT，第一次出现要写全称 + 缩写）
□ 自定义概念 / 术语（你 paper 自己造的词，如 "behavioral death"、"sycophancy score"）
□ 数学符号 / 公式中的变量（每个变量在第一次出现的方程里就要解释）
□ 自定义方法 / 模块组件（"the consistency gate"、"the alignment head"）
□ 评测指标（自定义的 metric，必须在 main result 表出现之前定义清楚）
□ 数据集 / setting 的内部命名（"the strict split"、"the holdout pool"）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
常见违规模式
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

❌ Abstract / Intro 里用了一个自造术语，Method 才定义
❌ Intro 提到 "we use SYCONScore"，到 §4 才看到这个 metric 的定义
❌ Method §2 用了变量 α，到 §2.3 才写 "where α denotes..."
❌ Method 提到 "module M"，结构图 + 解释在 Appendix 才有
❌ Table 1 列了 "Score@K"，K 在 §5 才说取什么值

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
修复策略
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

每个违规位置二选一：

A) **把定义提前**到第一次使用之前。最干净。
B) 在第一次使用的位置**加一个简短的 inline 解释**（一句话 / 括号说明）+ "(formally defined in §X)" 引用前向。容忍度更高的妥协方案。

注意：A 优先。只有 A 会改动太多结构时，才退而求其次用 B。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 找到多少处先用后定义？分布在哪些章节？
(b) 每一处用 A 还是 B 修的？
(c) 修完后 PDF 编译通过

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Psychometric Methods for LLM Evaluation.}
5	\looseness=-1
6	The Mantel-Haenszel procedure \citep{holland1988mh} remains the most widely used DIF detector in educational testing due to its simplicity and well-characterized statistical properties, operating nonparametrically without requiring an explicit item response theory (IRT) model. DIF analysis is a specific instance of measurement invariance testing \citep{meredith1993invariance}, which examines whether an instrument functions equivalently across groups. IRT adoption in LLM evaluation has accelerated since \citet{lalor2019irt} introduced it to natural language processing: recent work calibrates test sets for discriminability, item selection, and adaptive evaluation. The closest concurrent work is Growing Pains \citep{habba2026growingpains}, which uses parametric IRT (two-parameter logistic [2PL] / three-parameter logistic [3PL] fixed-parameter calibration) to equate scores across temporal cohorts. MH-DIF is nonparametric and robust to model misspecification, an advantage given poor 2PL fit for MMLU (root mean square error of approximation [RMSEA] $> 0.08$; \Cref{app:power_sensitivity}). Growing Pains prescribes how to equate scores after instability is detected; our work diagnoses \emph{which} items exhibit instability and quantifies severity via ETS classification, providing pre-screened anchor candidates for downstream equating. Their anchor-item selection can directly consume our stable-item set; our finding that 31\% of items exhibit temporal DIF identifies which items should \emph{not} serve as anchors. To our knowledge, no prior work applies DIF to benchmark temporal comparability.
7	
8	\paragraph{Contamination Detection.}
9	\looseness=-1
10	A growing family of methods detects benchmark contamination through model behavior or training-data analysis. Membership inference approaches use token-level log-likelihood statistics \citep{shi2024mink} or statistical anomalies \citep{dekoninck2024constat} to flag items likely seen during training. A standard validation practice is to compare detector output against web-overlap labels \citep{li2024contamination}, which classify items based on verbatim matches in publicly accessible corpora. Such labels measure \emph{global exposure} (whether an item exists in training data) rather than \emph{differential item functioning}: whether an item behaves differently across model cohorts. This global-vs-differential distinction motivates our comparison with web-overlap labels (\Cref{sec:groundtruth}). \citet{wang2026fragility} show that existing contamination detectors are fragile under reinforcement learning, further motivating behavioral approaches grounded in psychometric theory.
11	
12	\paragraph{Benchmark Quality and Design.}
13	\looseness=-1
14	Meta-evaluation of static benchmark quality~\citep{zhu2026bhi} assesses properties at a single snapshot; our temporal DIF analysis diagnoses \emph{when} measurement properties shift. Work on LLM memorization \citep{carlini2022quantifying} establishes that memorization depends on data frequency and model scale, reinforcing the distinction between global exposure and differential behavior. MMLU-CF~\citep{zhao2025mmlucf} reconstructs contamination-free items without replacement; DIF monitoring identifies which existing items remain measurement-invariant. Dynamic benchmarks~\citep{kiela2021dynabench} mitigate contamination by construction but lack psychometric equating. Broader surveys~\citep{chang2024survey} identify benchmark saturation as a systemic challenge that DIF analysis directly addresses.
15	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LoJwHnRXe7BQUAY1ErZfbU`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Every leaderboard comparison assumes that benchmark items measure the same construct across model generations. In educational testing, this foundational property, measurement invariance, is routinely verified. In large language model (LLM) evaluation, it has never been tested at scale. We bring differential item functioning (DIF) analysis from psychometrics to the METABENCH response matrix (5,227 models on MMLU) and find that 31\% of items function systematically differently between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts, 50$\times$ above random baselines. This instability is not driven by data contamination: DIF flags are near-independent of web-overlap labels ($\phi = 0.012$), an overlooked diagnostic dimension. Nor does it threaten validity: aggregate rankings remain near-perfect ($\rho \approx 0.997$). Instead, unstable items expose which capability dimensions shift across generations; base and instruct models exhibit opposite DIF directions. We develop a semi-synthetic validation framework with cross-subject matching that guarantees zero false-positive inflation, replicate findings across six benchmarks (18--31\% instability), and propose an actionable audit protocol. LLM benchmarks need psychometric monitoring, not just score reporting\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/irt_equated_bench_regen-2431/}.}.
3	\end{abstract}
4

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	When we say ``Model B outperforms Model A on MMLU,'' we assume that every test item measures the same construct for both models. In educational testing, this property, \emph{measurement invariance}~\citep{meredith1993invariance}, is routinely verified before scores are compared across groups; in LLM evaluation, it has never been tested at scale. We test it here, applying Mantel-Haenszel differential item functioning (DIF) analysis~\citep{holland1988mh} with ETS severity classification to the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench} (5,227 models $\times$ 12,508 MMLU~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu} items). The result is sobering: 31\% of items receive ETS Category~C classification ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$, $p < 0.05$) between 2023 and 2024 model cohorts, compared to 0.62\% under random-split controls, and the finding replicates across all six METABENCH benchmarks (17.9--31.3\%).
6	
7	\begin{figure*}[t]
8	\centering
9	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_0_pipeline.pdf}
10	\caption{Four-stage psychometric auditing pipeline. \textbf{\ding{192}} A binary response matrix records model correctness across benchmark items, with models split into temporal cohorts (2023 reference vs.\ 2024 focal). \textbf{\ding{193}} Models are stratified into $K{=}5$ score quintiles. \textbf{\ding{194}} Per-item Mantel-Haenszel DIF tests compute $\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}$ across strata. \textbf{\ding{195}} ETS classification assigns severity labels (A/B/C). Result: 31.3\% of items flagged as C (29.2\% after DEFF correction), yet stable-item $\rho = 0.997$, replicated across six benchmarks.}
11	\label{fig:pipeline}
12	\end{figure*}
13	
14	\looseness=-1
15	This instability is not what it appears. Aggregate rankings are near-perfect ($\rho \approx 0.997$): even after all unstable items are removed, the leaderboard barely changes. But individual items have shifted in structured ways: some became easier for newer models independent of overall ability, others harder, and these differential effects can shift individual models by ${\pm}561$ rank positions at the 99th percentile (\Cref{app:rank_reversal}). Instability does not threaten validity; it reveals which capability dimensions shift across model generations. Base and instruct models exhibit opposite DIF directions ($\chi^2 = 562.7$), which confirms structured capability changes, not noise.
16	
17	\looseness=-1
18	This diagnostic signal is distinct from what existing benchmark integrity methods target. Contamination detection asks whether items appear in training corpora, probing \emph{global exposure}~\citep{shi2024mink,dekoninck2024constat,liang2026dvd,tao2026selfcritique}, validated against web-overlap labels~\citep{li2024contamination}. Measurement invariance asks whether items \emph{function equivalently} across model cohorts (\Cref{fig:global_vs_differential}). We find these two signals are near-independent (Jaccard~$= 0.206$, $\phi = 0.012$): complementary diagnostic dimensions, not competing ones. Any item-level detector validated solely against web-overlap labels is blind to differential functioning.
19	
20	\begin{figure*}[t]
21	\centering
22	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_1_global_vs_differential.pdf}
23	\caption{Global vs.\ differential contamination via Item Characteristic Curves (ICCs). \textbf{Left}: global contamination produces no DIF; both cohorts encounter the item, and their ICCs overlap. \textbf{Right}: differential contamination produces DIF; the focal cohort's ICC shifts due to disproportionate exposure. The enrichment ratio of $\approx 1.0$ between DIF flags and web-overlap labels is expected because most items fall into the left category.}
24	\label{fig:global_vs_differential}
25	\end{figure*}
26	
27	\looseness=-1
28	To our knowledge, this is the largest-scale DIF analysis on LLM benchmarks, complementary to concurrent work on anchor-item calibration~\citep{habba2026growingpains} and motivated by the fragility of existing detectors under post-training perturbation~\citep{wang2026fragility}. We make three contributions:
29	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*,topsep=2pt]
30	\item \textbf{Temporal DIF at scale}: 31.3\% of MMLU items exhibit large temporal DIF (17.9--31.3\% across six benchmarks; 29.2\% after non-independence correction), near-independent of web-overlap labels (Jaccard $= 0.206$); base-vs-instruct stratification reveals opposite patterns ($\chi^2 = 562.7$), consistent with structured capability shifts rather than uniform exposure (\Cref{sec:temporal,sec:groundtruth,sec:gsm8k,sec:disc_temporal}).
31	\item \textbf{Semi-synthetic validation with $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0$ guarantee}: cross-subject matching eliminates false-positive inflation under single-subject contamination ($k \leq 5$), portable across benchmarks (\Cref{sec:semisynthetic}).
32	\item \textbf{DIF as diagnostic lens with actionable audit protocol}: unstable items carry disproportionate cross-cohort signal ($z = -8$ to $-49\sigma$) yet are the least ranking-disruptive under non-random removal, which confirms that DIF isolates differential functioning rather than generally informative items; we propose a Monitor/Triage/Correct protocol for benchmark maintainers (\Cref{sec:disc_protocol}).
33	\end{enumerate}
34	
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We adopt Differential Item Functioning (DIF) analysis from educational measurement to assess whether individual benchmark items behave consistently across groups of LLMs. The procedure requires only a binary response matrix and group labels, with no access to model internals or training data. Below we define the data, the statistical test, the cohort designs, and a semi-synthetic validation framework.
5	
6	\subsection{Data}
7	\label{sec:data}
8	
9	The primary dataset is the METABENCH response matrix~\citep{kipnis2024metabench}, which records binary outcomes (1~=~correct, 0~=~incorrect) for 5,227 models evaluated on 12,508 MMLU items~\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}. Models span release dates from 2023 to 2024, drawn from the Open LLM Leaderboard. MMLU covers 57 subjects grouped into five broad domains (STEM, Humanities, Social Sciences, Other, and a mixed category). We supplement the response matrix with the web-overlap contamination labels of \citet{li2024contamination}, which classify 9,229 of 12,508 items (73.8\% coverage) as ``contaminated'' or ``clean'' based on verbatim matches in publicly accessible web corpora. These labels serve as an external reference for evaluating the relationship between DIF flags and existing contamination indicators.
10	
11	\subsection{Mantel-Haenszel DIF and ETS Classification}
12	\label{sec:mhdif}
13	
14	In educational testing, Differential Item Functioning (DIF) occurs when an item's statistical relationship to the latent trait $\theta$ differs between two groups after conditioning on ability level. An item with DIF is not merely harder for one group; it functions differently relative to what the group's overall ability would predict. The standard terminology refers to test-takers as ``examinees'' and questions as ``items.'' In our setting, examinees are LLMs and items are benchmark questions.
15	
16	The Mantel-Haenszel (MH) procedure~\citep{holland1988mh,magis2010difr} detects DIF by stratifying examinees into $K$ score bins based on total score (a proxy for $\theta$) and computing a common odds ratio across strata. We use $K = 5$ equiprobable strata (quintile-based binning) following ETS recommended practice for large-scale assessments~\citep{donoghue1993mh,clauser1998dif}; $K{=}3$ yields near-identical power (\Cref{app:k3_power}). Within each stratum $k$, the responses of the reference group and the focal group form a $2 \times 2$ contingency table:
17	
18	\begin{equation}
19	\label{eq:mh}
20	\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}} = \frac{\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K} A_k D_k \,/\, T_k}{\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K} B_k C_k \,/\, T_k}\,,
21	\end{equation}
22	
23	\noindent where $A_k$ and $B_k$ are the number of correct and incorrect responses in the reference group for stratum $k$, $C_k$ and $D_k$ are the corresponding counts for the focal group, and $T_k$ is the total number of examinees in stratum $k$. Under the null hypothesis of no DIF, $\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}} = 1$. The log odds ratio is converted to the ETS delta scale:
24	
25	\begin{equation}
26	\label{eq:delta}
27	\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}} = -2.35 \ln(\alpha_{\mathrm{MH}})\,.
28	\end{equation}
29	
30	The Educational Testing Service (ETS) classifies DIF severity into three categories based on $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}|$. Category~A (negligible) requires $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| < 1.0$. Category~C (large) requires both $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ and statistical significance at $p < 0.05$ from the MH chi-squared test. Category~B (moderate) includes all remaining items: those with $1.0 \leq |\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| < 1.5$, or $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ without statistical significance. The dual threshold in ETS~C (effect size \emph{and} significance) guards against flagging items that show large but unreliable effect sizes. These thresholds were calibrated for human test-taker populations; we retain them as standardized reference points and verify robustness across stricter cutoffs (\Cref{sec:temporal}). Throughout this paper, we report the percentage of items classified as ETS~C, denoted C\%. At $N = 1{,}887$ (the temporal comparison; 5{,}227 total across all cohort designs), significance ($p < 0.05$) is nearly automatically satisfied for any $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$: only 3 of 3{,}918 C items lack significance, so the dual criterion effectively reduces to a single effect-size threshold; per-item DEFF correction (\Cref{app:deff}) restores $p$-value selectivity by reducing effective $N$: under simple-mean DEFF, 269 items (6.9\%) lose significance while retaining $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$; 74\% of these are near-miss ($p_{\mathrm{adj}} \in [0.05, 0.10]$).
31	
32	\begin{figure}[t]
33	\centering
34	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_ets_distribution.pdf}
35	\caption{Distribution of $\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}$ across 12,508 MMLU items (2023 vs.\ 2024 cohorts). Colors indicate ETS severity: A (negligible, $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| < 1.0$), B (moderate), C (large, $|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ and $p < 0.05$). The heavy tails show that 31.3\% of items exhibit large DIF, 50$\times$ above random baselines (\Cref{sec:temporal}).}
36	\label{fig:ets_distribution}
37	\end{figure}
38	
39	The choice of matching variable (the total score used for stratification) affects both validity and interpretation. We distinguish three matching strategies: (1)~\emph{standard matching} uses the within-benchmark total score on all items of the same benchmark; (2)~\emph{cross-subject matching}, adapting purified subtest matching~\citep{swaminathan1990logistic} to the LLM multi-subject setting, uses the total score on all subjects \emph{other than} the one containing the item under test, preventing the matching variable from absorbing injected contamination signals (\Cref{sec:injection}); and (3)~\emph{external matching} uses the total score on a different benchmark entirely (e.g., MMLU total score as the matching variable when testing DIF on Grade School Math 8K (GSM8K) items), providing an independent ability proxy uncontaminated by the target benchmark. Cross-subject and external matching both address matching variable contamination but at different granularities: cross-subject operates within a multi-domain benchmark, while external operates across benchmarks. Total score matching is adequate: IRT $\theta$ correlates at $r = 0.9986$ with total scores (\Cref{sec:structural}).
40	
41	\paragraph{LLM-specific challenges.} Applying MH-DIF to LLMs introduces four challenges absent in educational testing. First, overlapping training corpora violate local independence; permutation simulation (1{,}000 replications shuffling group labels within score strata) confirms local-dependence-attributable FPR inflation $< 0.001$~pp under cross-subject matching (\Cref{app:method_details}). Second, fine-tuned variants within model families are not independent examinees; graduated de-duplication shows family structure does not inflate C\% (\Cref{sec:structural}). Third, bimodal ability distributions motivate quintile-based stratification. Fourth, MMLU's 57-subject structure raises a multidimensionality concern~\citep{ackerman1992didmf,roussos1996multidimensionality}: domain-level ability profiles are nearly flat across cohorts (Cohen's $d$ range $= 0.12$), cross-subject and within-subject matching agree ($\kappa = 0.80$, 834 items), and cross-subject-unique C items do not concentrate in high-shift subjects (\Cref{app:structural}).
42	
43	\subsection{Cohort Designs}
44	\label{sec:cohorts}
45	
46	We construct five cohort comparisons: (1)~\emph{Temporal}: 2023 ($N = 1{,}080$) vs.\ 2024 ($N = 807$) models, the primary comparison; (2)~\emph{Ability-quintile}: Q1--Q2 vs.\ Q4--Q5 within the same temporal window; (3)~\emph{Within-family}: Llama-2 ($N = 161$) vs.\ Llama-3 ($N = 308$), isolating generational changes within a single family; (4)~\emph{Within-subject}: per-subject DIF to test domain concentration; and (5)~\emph{Random split}: two random halves of the 2023 cohort ($N = 540$ each) as a sanity check. Calendar-year boundaries are coarse; half-yearly comparison (C\% $= 34.4\%$; \Cref{app:uncertainty}) and base-only/instruct-only analyses (C\% $= 32.1\%$/$36.5\%$; \Cref{app:baseinst_temporal}) confirm DIF persists under finer slicing.
47	
48	\begin{figure}[t]
49	\centering
50	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_2_dose_response.pdf}
51	\caption{Semi-synthetic dose-response for DIF detection on MMLU. $\Delta\mathrm{TPR}$ ($= \mathrm{TPR}_{\text{injected}} - \mathrm{TPR}_{\text{baseline}}$) increases monotonically with exploitation rate $p$ under binary injection (a calibration upper bound; \Cref{sec:injection}); MH-DIF is sensitive to differential item behavior when present. Baseline (no injection) produces $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} \approx 0$.}
52	\label{fig:dose_response}
53	\end{figure}
54	
55	\subsection{Semi-Synthetic Injection Framework}
56	\label{sec:injection}
57	
58	We use contamination injection as a controlled case study to validate DIF detection power; DIF itself is mechanism-agnostic and flags items that function differently without attributing cause (\Cref{sec:disc_protocol}). To evaluate whether MH-DIF can detect differential contamination when it is present, we construct a semi-synthetic framework with controlled ground truth.
59	
60	\paragraph{Procedure.} From items classified as ETS~A in the uninjected data (negligible DIF), we select 20\% uniformly at random as the ``contaminated'' set. For each contaminated item, we flip incorrect responses to correct in the focal group at exploitation rate $p \in \{0.0, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$. We then run MH-DIF on the modified response matrix and measure detection performance. We report $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} = \mathrm{TPR}(\text{injected}) - \mathrm{TPR}(\text{baseline})$ and $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = \mathrm{FPR}(\text{injected}) - \mathrm{FPR}(\text{baseline})$, where TPR is the true positive rate on injected items and FPR is the false positive rate on uninjected items. Each injection level is repeated across 5 random seeds for uncertainty estimation. Because binary response flipping represents a stylized uniform injection pattern, reported $\Delta\mathrm{TPR}$ values may overestimate detection power; realistic contamination (partial memorization, format-specific advantages) would produce weaker signals.
61	
62	\paragraph{Cross-subject matching.} The choice of matching variable is critical for valid semi-synthetic evaluation. When contamination is injected into items within subject $X$, the total score on subject $X$ absorbs part of the injection signal. Using this contaminated total score as the MH matching variable violates the conditional independence assumption and inflates FPR. Cross-subject matching resolves this by computing the matching variable from all subjects \emph{other than} the one being tested.
63	
64	\paragraph{Remark (Zero FPR under single-subject matching).}
65	\label{prop:fpr}
66	Let $M_i^{(-s)} = \sum_{j \notin s} Y_{ij}$ be the matching variable for examinee $i$ on focal subject $s$. Under single-subject injection (contamination confined to subject $s$), the tested item belongs to $s$ and does not contribute to $M_i^{(-s)}$; injecting contamination into $s$ leaves $M_i^{(-s)}$ unchanged. Therefore $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0$ by construction.
67	
68	When contamination spans multiple subjects simultaneously, $M_i^{(-s)}$ may absorb signal from other contaminated subjects, potentially inflating FPR. Empirically, this guarantee extends to multi-domain contamination affecting $\leq$9\% of subjects ($k \leq 5$), with graceful degradation beyond this boundary (\Cref{sec:semisynthetic}).
69	
70	Empirically, $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0.000$ across all five MMLU domains (bootstrap 95\% CI $= [0.000, 0.000]$). $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0$ is a constructive guarantee of the matching strategy, independent of injection realism, whereas $\Delta\mathrm{TPR}$ may overestimate detection power under the stylized uniform injection. $\Delta\mathrm{FPR}$ remains negligible for single-domain contamination ($k \leq 5$); when contamination spans many domains, within-subject matching should be preferred (\Cref{sec:semisynthetic}). We adopt cross-subject matching as the standard protocol.
71	
72	Following DIF convention~\citep{wainer1993differential}, we do not apply family-wise error correction: the dual ETS~C threshold ($|\Delta_{\mathrm{MH}}| \geq 1.5$ \emph{and} $p < 0.05$) guards against false positives; Benjamini-Hochberg false discovery rate correction at $q = 0.05$ reclassifies only 3 of 3{,}918 C items (\Cref{app:method_details}). Diagnostic procedures (Yen's Q3, domain-specificity, DIF decomposition) and further details appear in \Cref{app:method_details}.
73	
74	\begin{figure}[t]
75	\centering
76	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_3_within_subject_matching.pdf}
77	\caption{Cross-subject vs.\ within-subject matching ($p = 0.5$). Each arrow connects the same subject under both strategies. Cross-subject points (filled) cluster at $\Delta\mathrm{FPR} = 0$ while retaining $\Delta\mathrm{TPR} > 0.3$ for 4/5 subjects; within-subject points (open) inflate FPR by up to ${+}0.12$.}
78	\label{fig:matching}
79	\end{figure}
80	
```
